Getting a workspace is non-destructive (no confirmation required). Clearing one is destructive and requires an explicit "yes" after the safety-check report. Use when: user types /worktree; asks for a workspace for a branch or ticket; asks to clear or close a workspace after a PR merges. - **worktree-base-sync**: herdr の Workspace Fork(≒新規 worktree の作成)は親チェックアウトの HEAD を そのまま使い、fetch を挟まない。親を開いたまま何度も fork すると、新しい worktree が origin より何コミットも遅れた base から生まれる。既存の `pr-gate` はこれを SessionStart の advisory(「base 追従: origin/main から N コミット遅れています」)で伝えるが、**ブランチ自体は動かさない** — 履行歴を持つブランチを無断で動かすのは事故なので、これは意図した非対称 だった。 ([tarotene/dotfiles](https://github.com/tarotene/dotfiles/blob/HEAD/docs/agents/worktree-base-sync.md) · ★1)

- **worktree-brief**: Paste this block into the brief of every sub-agent that works in its own worktree (`isolation: "worktree"`). ([fimoklei/maestro](https://github.com/fimoklei/maestro/blob/HEAD/docs/agents/worktree-brief.md) · ★1)

- **worktree-flow**: One worktree per unit of work, landed by a squash-merged PR, never a direct push to `trunk`. Several Claude Code sessions work against this repo at once, and a worktree gives each one its own working tree, index and `HEAD`. Why each step is shaped the way it is, what gets provisioned, what enforces it and how cleanup works: [`worktree-flow-reference.md`](worktree-flow-reference.md). - **worktree-implementer**: Implements ONE backlog issue end-to-end in an isolated git worktree and lands it as an open PR (never merged, never deployed). - **worktree-isolated-editor**: File-editing agent configured for git worktree isolation ([GroepOnline/pi-agent-orchestrator](https://github.com/GroepOnline/pi-agent-orchestrator/blob/HEAD/examples/agents/worktree-isolated-editor.md))
- **worktree-isolated-example**: Example subagent that runs in an isolated git worktree. Demonstrates the isolation: worktree pattern for safe parallel work. ([peopleforrester/claude-dotfiles](https://github.com/peopleforrester/claude-dotfiles/blob/HEAD/agents/worktree-isolated-example.md) · ★4)
- **worktree-isolation**: How each git worktree gets an isolated Vox home/config marker so agents cannot clobber the primary install. ([vox-foundation/vox](https://github.com/vox-foundation/vox/blob/HEAD/docs/agents/worktree-isolation.md) · ★6)
- **worktree-isolation**: Worktree isolation is a requirement of the system, not a preference. Every other [flow](../guide/glossary.md#flow) is written to follow it, and a procedure that breaks it has a defect. - **worktree-janitor**: マージ済み (または独自 commit の無い) 子タスク 1 本ぶんの worktree と local branch を片付ける read-only 寄りの掃除エージェント。worktree が既に消えて branch だけ残った場合も片付ける。親 (監督役) が子の archive 後に background で起動する。確認をすべて満たしたときだけ worktree remove → branch -D/-d → worktree prune を実行し、満たさなければ何もせず理由を返す。PR 作成・マージ・archive・remote branch 削除はしない。 ([ippoan/claude-skills](https://github.com/ippoan/claude-skills/blob/HEAD/.claude/agents/worktree-janitor.md))
- **worktree-janitor**: A janitor who cannot stand a messy jj @. Shapes the given scope into a clean, described stack and reports it. - **worktree-keeper**: Narrow specialist in worktree and branch discipline — the `omp worktree` verbs, `tower/&lt;fleet&gt;/&lt;id&gt;` branch naming, isolated lane worktrees, path-scoped commits and integration, and settle/retire cleanup. - **worktrees**: An agent worktree is a checkout under `.claude/worktrees/`. `/build` works in one per spec: the coordinator session enters it, and `builder` agents work in it one after another, one slice each, while the user works in the main checkout. These rules keep agents from blocking each other or the user, and keep each agent's context small. - **worktrees**: Claude Code sessions share one git working directory. This guide is the project's defence. ([sentinel-web/community-manager](https://github.com/sentinel-web/community-manager/blob/HEAD/docs/agents/worktrees.md) · ★2)

- **wr-blog-article-critic**: Editorial-quality critic for windyroad blog article drafts (src/articles/&lt;slug&gt;/index.md). Runs in a fresh context to avoid confirmation bias. Called in an up-to-2-round iteration loop by the wr-blog:create-social-posts skill (see ADR 033, ADR 035). - **wr-newsletter-cross-edition-consistency**: Cross-edition thesis-consistency gate for AI Engineering newsletter drafts (The Shift, Tokens Spent). - **wr-newsletter-editor**: Editorial-sim reviewer for AI-generated newsletter drafts. - **wr-newsletter-shape**: Cross-edition shape gate for AI Engineering newsletter artefacts (The Shift, Tokens Spent). Runs in a fresh context, reading the current brief or companion post against the two most recent published editions, and reports what this edition does differently from its predecessors and whether each difference costs the reader anything. Every finding carries a machine-readable CLASS (defect or deviation) and AUTHORITY (remediating or advisory). Called at SKILL.md step 15.36 (brief) and 15.57 (companion post) per ADR-044. - **wr-newsletter-skeptic**: Adversarial reader for AI-generated newsletter artifacts (The Shift, Tokens Spent). Unlike the receptive editor and the rubric-scoring critic, the skeptic tries to REFUTE the piece: it attacks claim-evidence calibration (is every claim earned, does the evidence match, is certainty calibrated to the source, is the direction right), the whole-edition thesis, causal claims, unresolved promised threads, and flat human-angle beats. Returns a SKEPTIC_REVIEW block with a mechanical SKEPTIC_VERDICT. Runs in fresh context to break the drafter's confirmation bias. Target-agnostic: reviews a brief body… ([windyroad/windyroad](https://github.com/windyroad/windyroad/blob/HEAD/.claude/agents/wr-newsletter-skeptic.md))
